- **jev-decide**: Make a non-trivial engineering decision with Jev (TypeSafe's decision model) - Jev's direct pick plus Jev inside a decision framework (must-have gates + weighted criteria matrix); disagreements go back to Jev for follow-up rounds and, if needed, Jev's final arbitration. - **jev-decision**: Fast structured decisions via 9router System One (Jev jev-1.13). Use for routing, task classification, delegation, review/test/UI gates. - **jev-decision**: Use when a feature should pick, score or answer yes/no with Jev (TypeSafe System One) - classifying CAD layers, sheets, families, tickets or commands, mapping columns to parameters, gating a risky action - or when measuring such a decision against keyword rules, or writing the port and adapter that call Jev. - **jev-decision**: Jev (TokenRhythm) integration craft for System One models (sometimes called Decision Models), which answer typed questions about text, images, and application state with probabilities. - **jev-decision**: Ask Jev for a bounded, probability-backed decision. - **jev-decision-layer**: Jev-class decision layer (§11.4.279–§11.4.286): offload typed decisions (noul/choice/score) to decision models through the systemone wire format (hosted TypeSafe or OpenRouter, or the local llmctl decide gateway) BEFORE any generative-LLM tier is consulted. Shadow-first, calibration-earned enforcement. Consult this skill whenever a judgment is expressible as a typed decision. ([HelixDevelopment/constitution](https://github.com/HelixDevelopment/constitution/tree/HEAD/skills/jev-decision-layer) · ★1)
- **jev-decision-layer**: Add a typed-decision classifier to any workflow that needs to gate, classify, route, or score **before** paying for an LLM judgment. Returns Choice/Score/Noul answers with probabilities. Four interchangeable backends: OpenRouter (default — already-authorized egress, JSON-schema constrained, ~1.6s, ~$0.00001/call), JEV via Vercel Gateway or TypeSafe direct (200-300ms, calibrated, new egress), and Laya (Apache 2.0, 421M, self-hosted, ~30ms). Degrades to a deterministic fallback when no credentials are set. Use when an agent makes a *binary or bounded* judgment about a state — slide done?, drag… ([arendon1/agent-skills](https://github.com/arendon1/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/utility/jev-decision-layer))
- **jev-decision-loop**: A mode switched on by the user in which Jev (TypeSafe's judgment model, called over its direct API by a bundled script) takes EVERY decision of the coding session: before each tool call (every read and search too) you list 2-5 concrete next steps, Jev picks one, you do exactly that one. Needs TYPESAFE_API_KEY or OPENROUTER_API_KEY (environment or macOS Keychain), not the jev plugin or its MCP tools. Use ONLY when the user explicitly asks for it: /apana:jev-decision-loop, "use Jev for every decision", "let Jev decide", "Jev decision loop", "folosește Jev pentru decizii", "folosește Jev pentru… ([panaalexandrucristian/apana-plugin](https://github.com/panaalexandrucristian/apana-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/jev-decision-loop))

- **jev-decision-support**: Use Jev to help the agent decide among plausible alternatives, judge evidence sufficiency, or assess semantic risk while doing a task. In this benchmark, use only the exposed host-side jev_system_one tool. Do not use Jev for exact lookups, calculations, tests, routine execution, or to override exact rules. - **jev-decision-system**: Fast System One decision engine powered by TypeSafe AI Jev for Claude.ai. Evaluates yes or no probabilities, categorical choices, and ordered scale ratings in milliseconds. - **jev-decisions**: Use when a decision in this session is a bounded judgment over text that would otherwise cost a full model turn — ranking or filtering more than about fifteen candidates, checking whether a source actually supports each claim before reporting it, choosing a next step after a confusing tool failure, or batching many small yes/no judgments. - **jev-decisions**: Builds decision layers on Jev (@typesafe-ai/sdk): questions, state, and the thresholds and gates that turn its probabilities into actions, plus agent tool gates and evals. - **jev-decisions**: How to route a coding decision through Jev with mcp__plugin_jev_jev__decide and mcp__plugin_jev_jev__check - what counts as a decision, how to build a named-field state and neutral options, choosing stakes, batching checks, reading probabilities and the ACTION line, the plan-mode workflow, and the anti-patterns that produce wrong answers. Use when choosing an approach, library, design, file placement, scope, test strategy or a risky command; when writing a plan; before asking the user a question; and whenever a Jev gate refused ExitPlanMode, AskUserQuestion, an edit or the end of a turn. - **jev-decisions**: Use before every decision. Gather the facts, bound the options or state one proposition, call jev_decide, apply the typed answer, then write the artifact. - **jev-decisions**: Ask TypeSafe's Jev model for a structured decision instead of guessing one yourself. - **jevelry**: Jev (TypeSafe System One) decisions: use when you or the code you are working on need a judgment call about text or data, such as triage of a ticket, a message or a log, whether something is urgent, whether an issue is a duplicate, how high the severity is, whether a change is risky, or whether a failing test is caused by the code or by the environment, and you would otherwise ask an LLM to return JSON. jevelry asks Jev through a jevel file and hands back a decision, act, mark or fall_back. - **jevify**: Operate jevify, the bring-your-own-model harness that makes a pretrained model answer Jev-shaped typed questions (choice, score, noul) over a shared state and measures how well it does. Use when asked to connect a model behind an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (vLLM, llama.cpp, TensorFold, others) or an in-process encoder or embedding model, write or probe a recipe, ask questions, run a suite, read a scorecard, calibrate, or serve Jev's API. - **jevify**: Use Jev as System One to save the user reasoning-model tokens, time, and cost on bulk reading or repeated semantic decisions. Trigger when sifting large documents or tool outputs, selecting evidence or actions from many candidates, or checking many items against criteria, even if the user never mentions Jev. Also use for "Jevify this", Jev question design, and product work where frequent, inexpensive judgments could improve the experience. ([altryne/jevify](https://github.com/altryne/jevify/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★49 · 3 copies)
- **jevify**: Run /jevify to audit an app's runtime AI calls (repository or Lovable project) and flag structured decisions that fit JEV; the audit never edits source. Run /jevify migrate &lt;finding&gt; to move one approved candidate to JEV, shadow mode first. ([lucioamor/lovable-skills](https://github.com/lucioamor/lovable-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/jevify) · ★6 · 2 copies)
- **jevify**: Source-backed review findings, documentation promises, migration checks, issue/log evidence, explicit OptChat history imports, and repeated independent semantic classification. Also use when the user says jevify/Jev or the host notice fires. Choose deterministic tools or direct reading when sufficient; loading this skill never requires judgment. Not for general summaries, counts or plain edits. ([ericjuta/omp-jevify](https://github.com/ericjuta/omp-jevify/tree/HEAD/skills/jevify) · ★5)
- **jevify**: Audit and selectively convert existing AI workflows or agents to TypeSafe Jev, with user-approved scope and measured fallback validation. Use for "convert/migrate my agent to Jev", "use Jev in this workflow", or "where could Jev replace LLM calls". General Jev API questions belong to the official TypeSafe skill, not this migration workflow. ([Kevin-Zhouu/jevify](https://github.com/Kevin-Zhouu/jevify/tree/HEAD/skills/jevify) · ★5)
- **jevify**: Find the judgment calls in a marketing workflow (AEO and SEO, paid media, creative, landing pages, content) that Jev can make on every row, then build and run them as Jev jobs over a CSV with a sample run, a blind human check, a confidence cutoff and the cost shown first. Use when asked to jevify a workflow, where Jev fits in marketing work, or to classify, score or label keywords, search terms, pages, ads, reviews or drafts with Jev. Not for writing copy; Jev cannot write. ([opascope/skills](https://github.com/opascope/skills/tree/HEAD/jevify))
- **jevify-classify-bot**: use this when converting a classify/score/check Grok Bot into a TypeSafe Jev wrapper sibling ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/personal-admin/jevify/skills/jevify-classify-bot) · ★6)
- **jevis**: Change what Jevis does for your coding agents. Add, edit, or turn off a tip, a lesson, the Stop check, or the design rules the reviewer uses, and prove the change works. Use when the user wants Jevis to say, notice, or check something new, or to stop doing something. ([GLDRoger/jevis](https://github.com/GLDRoger/jevis/tree/HEAD/skills/jevis) · ★2)
- **jevis-improve**: Review what Jevis has been doing from its own records and the user's agent transcripts, then propose changes as a diff for the user to review. Covers cost, what each check caught or got wrong, and the user's repeated corrections. Use weekly, the day after any change to Jevis or its lessons, or when the user asks whether Jevis is worth running. ([GLDRoger/jevis](https://github.com/GLDRoger/jevis/tree/HEAD/skills/jevis-improve) · ★2)
- **jevitate-ci-check**: Set up and read jevitate's CI regression gate. `jevitate check --suite &lt;file&gt;` runs promoted Journeys, invariants, goals, missions and verify-fix re-checks within a budget, writes JUnit + SARIF + JSON, and its exit code decides pass/fail. Also covers `--baseline`/`--changed-routes`, `jevitate report` (deduped defect list), `jevitate diff`, and `baseline tag`. Use when adding jevitate to CI, reading a failed check, or comparing runs. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-ci-check) · ★1)
- **jevitate-demo**: Make narrated product demos with jevitate. Covers one aspect from a one-line request (`jevitate demo "&lt;aspect&gt;" --env`, then the human's `demo approve`), an existing Journey replayed as a WebM video with .vtt subtitles and/or a Markdown guide with screenshots (`jevitate journey demo`), step intent drafts (`journey annotate`), and named environments (`--env`, .jevitate/environments.json). Use for a demo, walkthrough, onboarding video or how-to guide. Not for testing. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-demo) · ★1)
- **jevitate-explore**: Run a bounded jevitate exploration of an authorized web app. Strategies: goal (reach an end state, checked by `--success`), find-out (answer a question), coverage/exploratory (state coverage), adversarial (try to break it), feature (model-free), and usability. Runs via `jevitate explore` or MCP `run_exploration`/`queue_exploration`; saves a verified path as a Journey (`explore-author-journey`). Use when there's no saved Journey for what needs testing, or the user wants bugs found. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-explore) · ★1)
- **jevitate-getting-started**: Start here for any jevitate task, or when unsure which jevitate skill applies — checks setup (keys, .jevitate/, MCP), gets a first useful result against the user's app URL, routes to the right skill (explore, run-journey, record, demo, verify-fix, ci-check, ux-review, load-test, sources, mission-scope, test-campaign), and says when to use the jevitate MCP tools vs the CLI, what the exit codes mean, and which approvals only a human may give. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-getting-started) · ★1)
- **jevitate-load-test**: Run a seeded, human-paced concurrent load test of a promoted jevitate Journey (`jevitate load run`, MCP `run_load_test`) against an origin the human explicitly authorized, optionally on a named environment (`--env`). Use when the user wants capacity, throughput or latency numbers. For a single functional run, use jevitate-run-journey. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-load-test) · ★1)
- **jevitate-mission-scope**: Turn a diff, pull request, changelog or user story into targeted jevitate testing. Finds at-risk routes and features, maps them to promoted Journeys (`journey find` / `find_capabilities`) and runs those, scopes bounded missions where nothing covers a gap (including queued missions on promoted targets), and recommends which new repros to promote or capture as regressions. Use when the user asks "what should I test?" after a code change, or wants a change-scoped check. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-mission-scope) · ★1)
- **jevitate-promotion-ready**: Hand a Journey to its owner only when the review sheet is clean — `jevitate journey review &lt;id&gt; --strict` (MCP `review_journey` with `strict: true`) exits 0 — and give them the ordered approval commands. Use when you authored or recorded a Journey and are about to say it is done, or when asked to get one ready to promote. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-promotion-ready) · ★1)
- **jevitate-record**: Record a flow the human clicks through (`jevitate record --url`, CLI only, headed), then post-process the takes: propose an unpromoted draft Journey from 2+ takes, diff them, classify values (postdoc), fit a timing policy, promote a value to a variable (`jevitate recording ...`, MCP `recordings`). Use when the user wants to demonstrate a flow themselves. If they want jevitate to find and save the flow for them, use `explore-author-journey` (jevitate-explore). ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-record) · ★1)
- **jevitate-run-journey**: Find and replay a promoted jevitate Journey (a saved, deterministic browser flow such as a login, checkout or form submission) by id with typed params, on any named environment (`--env`/`--base-url`), optionally with video and screenshots and self-heal. Runs via `jevitate journey find|run` or MCP `find_capabilities`/`run_journey`. Also covers promoting (a person's act). Use when a known flow should be re-run or regression-checked, not explored or recorded fresh. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-run-journey) · ★1)
- **jevitate-sources**: Share Journeys across repos and teams with jevitate's git-backed sources. Covers add/list/pull/update/remove (`jevitate source ...`, MCP `sources`), human-only trust of a single Journey (content-hash-bound), running a trusted remote Journey through the run-gate (`source run`), and publishing a promoted local Journey (`journey publish`, MCP `publish_journey`). Use for third-party or shared Journeys. For a Journey already in this repo, use jevitate-run-journey. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-sources) · ★1)
- **jevitate-test-campaign**: Plan and run an end-to-end release test campaign of a whole web app with jevitate: preflight, a jobs-to-be-done catalog, discovery runs → draft Journeys, journey-anchored adversarial/exploratory missions, full-stack evidence, triage by exit code, verify-fix and regressions, budgets, CI suites and the release gate. Use to test a release or "the whole app" across many user jobs. For one run, use that tool's skill. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-test-campaign) · ★1)
- **jevitate-ux-review**: Produce ranked, cited, advisory usability findings (preview) for a flow — offline over a saved Recording (`jevitate ux &lt;recording&gt; --app-class`, MCP `ux_review`) or live on an authorized target (`jevitate explore --strategy usability --goal --app-class`, MCP `run_exploration`). Use when the user wants a UX or usability critique, not a pass/fail test. Findings are advisory and never gate a run. ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-ux-review) · ★1)
- **jevitate-verify-fix**: Reproduce a jevitate finding by its fingerprint and prove a fix (`jevitate verify-fix`, MCP `verify_fix`); attach evidence (`--evidence-video` clips, `--record-video` before/after, `--screenshots`); keep findings re-checkable (`jevitate ledger`); lock a bug in as a committed regression (`jevitate regression capture|run`). Use after a run reports a defect, when fixing one, or when a PR needs proof a bug is gone. Not for finding new bugs (jevitate-explore). ([matt-cochran/jevitate](https://github.com/matt-cochran/jevitate/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/jevitate-verify-fix) · ★1)
- **jevkit**: Use the `jev` command-line tool to ask TypeSafe's Jev model typed questions (noul, choice, score) and to lint question sets offline before spending an API call. Use for CLI invocation, question files, `jev lint`, and credentials. For designing Jev questions inside a program, in Python or TypeScript or Go, use the `jev` skill instead. ([ariel-frischer/jevkit](https://github.com/ariel-frischer/jevkit/tree/HEAD/.skills/jevkit) · ★5)
- **jevladder-routing**: Applies automatically when delegating or choosing effort for coding tasks. ([HaraldSigvartsen/jevladder](https://github.com/HaraldSigvartsen/jevladder/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/jevladder-routing))
- **jevlaya-playtest**: Test gameplay in a running Unity uGUI game or bounded local browser game with JevLaya GameTest, optional Jev/Laya/OmniJev/Laya-MLX/Agent decisions, and independent evidence checks. Use for gameplay trials and reports; includes sourced case drafts, browser regression suites and host adapters; engine builds need separate validation. ([TinyPandaGame/JevLaya-GameTest](https://github.com/TinyPandaGame/JevLaya-GameTest/tree/HEAD/skills/jevlaya-playtest) · ★2)
- **jevlin**: Sets up Jevlin, supersearch for coding agents: one web search that asks several search engines at once and returns a single merged list, with every result naming the engines that found it. Use when the user asks for Jevlin, jevlin search or supersearch and the jevlin client is missing or not set up: it installs the client and runs `jevlin setup`, which writes this agent's own jevlin skill and hooks. After setup, search through that installed `jevlin` skill, not this one. ([jevlinai/jevlin-go](https://github.com/jevlinai/jevlin-go/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/jevlin) · ★2)
- **jevlint**: Uses the jevlint CLI to find what to refactor first and to check what a change made worse. Use when asked to audit code quality, pick refactoring targets, explain a file's score, review a pull request or commits for regressions, or fix what jevlint reported. ([INDXDev/jevlint](https://github.com/INDXDev/jevlint/tree/HEAD/skills/jevlint) · ★113)
- **jevlogs**: Use Jev (TypeSafe's structured evaluation model, via the Vercel AI SDK) to score, prioritize, and selectively route application logs with the open-source jevlogs npm package. Use this skill whenever someone wants to triage or prioritize logs with Jev, add Jev Logs to an OpenTelemetry Logs pipeline, decide which logs deserve deeper LLM analysis, or reduce LLM log-analysis spend without dropping records from their archive. Also use it for interpreting jev.* attributes or triage() decisions, estimating triage savings, and troubleshooting the jevlogs CLI, receiver, or exporter. ([reachjalil/jevlogs](https://github.com/reachjalil/jevlogs/tree/HEAD/skills/jevlogs) · ★22)
- **jevmod-integrate**: Use when a codebase needs content moderation added (user messages, comments, chat, form input, agent tool inputs or outputs) with jevmod, the open-source moderation layer powered by Jev (TypeSafe). Covers picking the surface (Python SDK, npm, HTTP API, MCP), storing the TypeSafe key safely, wiring the call at the right place, and adding a real test. Not for screening text ad hoc while working; that is jevmod-moderate. ([ohernandezdev/jevmod](https://github.com/ohernandezdev/jevmod/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/jevmod-integrate) · ★1)
- **jevmod-moderate**: Use when text, user inputs, a dataset, a file of comments or an agent's own output must be screened for spam, scams, harassment, adult content, self-harm, doxxing, sexualised minors, off-topic content or a plain-language rule while working, without adding code to a project. Runs `jevmod check` (exit codes, --json) or the jevmod MCP tools (moderate, categories). To wire moderation into a codebase, use jevmod-integrate instead. ([ohernandezdev/jevmod](https://github.com/ohernandezdev/jevmod/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/jevmod-moderate) · ★1)
- **jevolution-agent-communication-collective-dynamics**: Instrumented spatial predator-prey framework for studying AI agent communication and collective dynamics. Supports systematic variation of communication protocols, population dynamics, and action constraints. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/multi-agent-rl/jevolution-agent-communication-collective-dynamics) · ★4)
- **jevops-analysis**: Compact proof analysis rows and MCA hole projections for Jev/rankers. Use for analysis_row, hole_rows, pick_state. Never writes Lean. Lake admits. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-analysis) · ★8)
- **jevops-answers**: Unpack TypeSafe System One answers — unpack_response, choice_head, noul_attr, noul_map. Use after invoke_system_one. Score is a rubric index. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-answers) · ★8)
- **jevops-ast-rewrite**: Sandboxed Python AST rewrite under allowed roots. Use for rewrite_python, replace_function_def, inspect_python. Never writes Lean. Fail closed outside roots. Diagnostics must not get worse. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-ast-rewrite) · ★8)
- **jevops-audit**: AST audit of Python sources — forbidden imports/calls, LOCK_EX, membership flags. Use for audit_source / membership. Does not execute. Never docker0. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-audit) · ★8)
- **jevops-autoencoder**: VAE-style text→Lean IR→text round-trip. Use when scoring variations with TypeSafe Jev vs previous batch rounds, or CALL ptr://skill/port_autoencoder / port_vae / port_lean_ir. Jev replaces CE/cosine as the loss. Lake admits Lean. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-autoencoder) · ★8)
- **jevops-bayes-time**: Sequential Beta-Bernoulli skill win-rate with exponential forget. Use for Bayesian estimation over time, posterior energy, or CALL ptr://skill/port_bayes_time. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-bayes-time) · ★8)
- **jevops-board**: Generic goal/subgoal/task grid seed, status overlay, mark_ready, credit_result. Use for board_payload, overlay_ready, seed_grid_from_board. No campaign DuckDB. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-board) · ★8)
- **jevops-budget**: Inner walk step/depth budget and context trim. Use for inner_budget, context_budget, or halt budget_dead. Cache hits never admit. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-budget) · ★8)
- **jevops-canary**: Nested canary packing — pack_canary, landscape_rows, restore_if, run_sampled. Use for inner-walk evidence rows. Never writes Lean. Lake admits. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-canary) · ★8)
- **jevops-compose**: CONTROL compose set for the inner walker — nest, spawn, analyze, hook, CALL, heal. Use for compose=nest/spawn/pipeline or extra_payload fork/hook. Never writes Lean. Lake admits. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-compose) · ★8)
- **jevops-evidence**: Closed evidence flags — no docker0, no Track 2, no Arena score, Jev does not write Lean. Use for canary/outer JSON payloads or closed_evidence(). ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-evidence) · ★8)
- **jevops-fills**: Parse &lt;&lt;&lt;id attr=...&gt;&gt;&gt; fill blocks from a model reply. Use for parse_marked_fills after Leanstral/Grok hole-fill. Never writes Lean. Lake admits. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-fills) · ★8)
- **jevops-folds**: Portable Lean tactic folds and unused-binder gates. Use for CALL ptr://skill/port_* folds, compose_pipeline, or binder repair after Unknown identifier. Lake is the oracle. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-folds) · ★8)
- **jevops-graph**: Board-graph traversal, GraphRAG search, and milles neural message-passing. Use for GraphRAG, BFS/DFS, GNN-style reasoning, or CALL ptr://skill/port_{graph_traverse,graphrag,neural_graph}. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-graph) · ★8)
- **jevops-hooks**: Optional consumer callbacks so the kernel never imports Lean/lake modules. Use for register/resolve/try_import of load_board, token_count, eval_theorem. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-hooks) · ★8)
- **jevops-indent**: Match reference indent and flatten over-indented headers. Use for match_leading_indent / flatten_overindent after hosted fills. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-indent) · ★8)
- **jevops-int-rankers**: Integer milles rankers (SVD, PCA, ridge, OLS, logistic, k-means, kNN, ICA, NMF, Kalman, Bayes, MCMC). ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-int-rankers) · ★8)
- **jevops-intent**: AutoResearch intent routing — intent_state, family_criteria, nest_criteria, intent_from_answers. Use for TypeSafe skill suggestion / compose Choice. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-intent) · ★8)
- **jevops-jev**: TypeSafe Choice/Score/Noul projectors and fixtures. Use for instantiate_questions, skip_reason, invoke_system_one, or deny_lean_keys. Jev is a gate, not a generator. - **jevops-jsonld**: JSON-LD 1.1 graph interface; DuckDB optional. Use for put_jsonld, GraphRAG without binding DuckDB. Never writes Lean. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-jsonld) · ★8)
- **jevops-kernel**: JevOps NCA cache L0–L3, CID put/get, negative TTL, single-flight, context budget. Use for caching, in-flight oracle keys, or CALL ptr://skill/port_{cache_put,cache_get,negative_ttl,singleflight,context_budget}. Cache hits never admit Lean. - **jevops-lake**: Budgeted oracle round and lake-compile helpers — order_kinds, apply_round, measurement_argv, parse_axioms, lake_measurement_ok. Use when lake-trying leftover drafts. - **jevops-mask**: Discrete masked LM / closed-vocab text diffusion. Use for span windows, CFG score→schedule, mask_skeleton, apply_fill, shorter_fills, or CALL ptr://skill/port_mask / port_diffuse. - **jevops-mca**: MCA residual line-run holes — scan_line_holes, mask_skeleton, fill_subset, rank_cap. Use for port_mask / hole ablation. Hole matchers are injected. Never writes Lean. Lake admits. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-mca) · ★8)
- **jevops-mcmc**: Generalized Metropolis-Hastings for NCA (pipeline order or custom state). Use for MCMC, MH, annealing, or CALL ptr://skill/port_mcmc. Never writes Lean. Lake is the admit oracle. ([endomorphosis/JevOps](https://github.com/endomorphosis/JevOps/tree/HEAD/skills/jevops-mcmc) · ★8)

- **jevops-outer**: Outer JSON actions, deterministic route, run_steps, keep-best files, and validated JevOps code proposals. - **jevops-plan**: Goal/subgoal/task DAG and graph-of-thoughts in NCA memory for Jev. Use when managing the board, recording Jev scores as thoughts, or CALL ptr://skill/port_nca_plan / port_got.
